--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Mission Analysis:**
- Score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region (BLUE-bordered strip with BLACK floor)
- Target: get all robots to the BLACK-floored strip near BLUE walls
- The other BLACK strip has GREEN walls
- Robots need to distinguish: BLACK floor near BLUE walls (target) vs BLACK floor near GREEN walls (non-target)
- GRAY floor is in between (not target)

**Key Perceptions:**
- Floor color: BLACK (both strips), GRAY (middle)
- Camera: can see BLUE walls (target side) or GREEN walls (non-target side)
- Neighbor count via range-and-bearing

**Strategy:**
- Robots should move toward BLUE color (GoToColor with blue=3) to reach the target strip
- When on BLACK floor and seeing BLUE, they should stop (they're in target region)
- When on GRAY or seeing GREEN, they should move toward BLUE walls
- Need to handle the case where robots are on the wrong BLACK strip (near GREEN walls)

**State Design:**

**FSM 1 (2 states - simple GoToBlue):**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) - moves toward blue walls
- State 1: Stop - when perceiving blue and on black floor
- Transition 0→1: floor BLACK + perceiving BLUE
- Transition 1→0: floor GRAY

**FSM 2 (2 states):**
- State 0: Exploration
- State 1: GoToColor(blue)
- Transition based on floor/color perception

**FSM 3 (3 states):**
- State 0: GoToColor(blue=3) - seek blue walls
- State 1: Stop - at target (black floor + blue visible)
- State 2: GoAwayColor(green=2) - avoid green walls

Let me design varied machines:

Color codes: blue=3, green=2, red=1

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

**Critical correction:** LED colour values are only {0, 4, 5, 6} — I was using 3 (blue) which is invalid. I'll use 0 (off), 4 (yellow), 5 (magenta), or 6 (cyan) instead.

**Mission recap:**
- Minimize robots outside the BLUE-bordered BLACK strip
- Target: BLACK floor + BLUE walls visible = target region
- Other BLACK strip has GREEN walls (wrong strip)
- GRAY floor = middle (not target)

**Strategy:**
- GoToColor(blue=3) to seek blue walls
- Stop when on BLACK floor AND seeing blue (in target)
- GoAwayColor(green=2) to avoid green walls
- Use floor color transitions (BLACK=0, GRAY=1) to detect position

**Topology designs:**

1. Simple 2-state: GoToBlue → Stop on black floor
2. 2-state: GoToBlue with transitions on floor+color perception
3. 3-state: Explore → GoToBlue → Stop
4. 3-state: GoToBlue → Stop → GoAwayGreen
5. 4-state: full pipeline

Let me be careful about LED colors: only 0, 4, 5, 6 allowed.

## Reasoning

The errors say I'm still using invalid LED colour values (3 = blue is not allowed). Looking back at my Round 3 answers, I see I correctly used 0, 4, 5, 6 in most places - but the error message repeats. Let me check very carefully: the allowed values are {0, 4, 5, 6} only. I must not use 1, 2, 3 for `--cle`.

Note: `--clr` (target colour for GoToColor/GoAwayColor) uses {1,2,3,4,5,6} - that's different from `--cle` (LED colour). I need to make sure I don't confuse these.

I have no score data from the runs yet (no best-ever reported), so I'll design a diverse set of machines based on mission analysis:

**Core insight:**
- GoToColor with clr=3 (blue) moves robots toward blue walls = target
- Stop on black floor (condition C=0) = robot is in strip (either target or non-target)
- Also check if blue is visible (C=7, l=3) to confirm it's the right strip
- GoAwayColor with clr=2 (green) pushes away from wrong strip

**All LED values will be strictly from {0, 4, 5, 6}.**

## Reasoning

The error keeps saying I'm using invalid LED value 3, but looking at my Round 4 answers I only used 0 and 4. This is very confusing. Let me re-read the error carefully.

Wait - the errors say rounds 2, 3, 4 all have the same errors. These might be cached/repeated errors from Round 2 that I haven't fixed properly in the simulator's view. OR perhaps the system is reporting errors from the machines that were actually run (which included my Round 2 bad submissions).

Let me be absolutely certain: `--cle<I>` must only use values from {0, 4, 5, 6}. I will use ONLY 0 in all my machines to be safe.

Also, I have no score feedback yet - no best performers reported. I'll continue with diverse designs.

**Key design principles:**
- GoToColor(clr=3) = move toward blue walls (target)
- Stop when on BLACK floor = in a strip (hopefully target)
- GoAwayColor(clr=2) = move away from green walls
- Camera condition C=7 with l=3 (blue) or l=2 (green) distinguishes strips

I'll use ONLY `--cle 0` for all LED settings to avoid any possibility of error.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The system keeps reporting the same errors about `--cle` value 3. I'm clearly NOT using value 3 in my recent submissions (I've been using only 0), yet the error persists. This suggests the error report may be stale/cached from Round 2, OR there's something else I'm missing.

Wait - let me re-read: "Allowed: 0, 4, 5, 6" for `--cle`. I see I wrote things like `--cle0 0` which should be fine. But wait - could there be a parsing issue where `--clr0 3` is being confused with `--cle0 3`? No, those are different flags.

Let me look more carefully at my submissions. In Round 3, I wrote:
`--s1 1 --cle1 0` - that's fine
But wait - in some machines I used `--cle1 4` - is 4 allowed? Yes, 4=yellow is allowed.

I think the issue might be that the error report is simply being recycled from Round 2 where I genuinely used `--cle 3`. The simulator may be showing historical errors. I'll continue with only {0, 4, 5, 6} for all `--cle` values.

Since I have no score data reported (no best-ever shown), I'll maintain diverse designs. I'll use only `--cle 0` throughout to be absolutely safe.

## Reasoning

The error keeps repeating identically across rounds, which strongly suggests these are stale errors from Round 2 being re-reported. My Round 3-6 submissions only used `--cle` values of 0 and 4, which are valid. I'll continue using only 0.

Since I have no score feedback (no best-ever reported), I need to reconsider my approach. The fact that no scores are being reported suggests either all my machines are erroring out (unlikely since I fixed the LED issue) OR the feedback format just doesn't show scores in this conversation.

Let me reconsider the mission more carefully:
- **AGGREGATION mission**: robots should aggregate (cluster together), not necessarily go to the blue strip
- Wait, re-reading: "score increases by 1 for every robot OUTSIDE the target region" - this IS about getting to the blue-bordered strip
- The target region is the BLACK-floored strip near BLUE walls

But wait - the title says "AGGREGATION mission" in the round headers. Let me re-read the mission description from Round 1... The mission IS to get robots to the blue strip (minimize robots outside target region).

My strategies seem correct. Let me try more varied approaches including using attraction behaviors to cluster robots, which might help them collectively navigate to the blue strip.

Key behaviors to try:
- GoToColor(blue=3): navigate toward blue walls
- Stop on black floor: stay in target strip
- Attraction: cluster robots together
- GoAwayColor(green=2): avoid wrong strip

I'll use ONLY `--cle 0` for all states.

## Reasoning

The same error keeps appearing every round. I am absolutely certain I'm not using `--cle` value 3 in my recent submissions. Yet the error persists. 

Let me look very carefully at my Round 7 submission for FSM 9:
`--s1 8 --vel1 1.0 --clr1 3 --cle1 0`

Wait - `--clr1 3` sets the GoToColor target to blue (color index 3). That's a valid `--clr` value. But could the parser be confusing `--clr1` with `--cle1`? That seems unlikely.

OR - could the issue be that `--c<I>x<K>` condition type values are being confused? No...

Actually, I wonder if the error message is specifically about my FSM with `--att0 3.0` - could `3.0` be parsed as an LED value somewhere? No, that's `--att0`.

I think the errors ARE stale from Round 2. My machines are running fine. Without score feedback I'll continue with my best guesses.

Let me try a completely fresh approach - maybe simpler is better. The key insight: robots need to reach the BLACK floor near BLUE walls. GoToColor(blue) is the most direct approach. Let me also try approaches that don't use GoToColor and instead use floor color + random exploration.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The error keeps repeating identically. I'm convinced this is a stale error being re-reported from Round 2. My machines since Round 3 have only used `--cle` values of 0 and 4.

However, I notice I have NO score feedback at all - no "best-ever" machine is reported, no scores shown. This is very strange. It's possible my machines are ALL failing to run due to some systematic error I'm not seeing.

Let me think harder about what could be wrong. The error says `--cle` value 3 is invalid. Could there be a different parameter that's causing issues? Let me trace through one of my machines very carefully:

`--nstates 2 --s0 8 --vel0 1.0 --clr0 3 --cle0 0 --n0 1 --n0x0 0 --c0x0 0 --p0x0 0.9 --s1 1 --cle1 0 --n1 1 --n1x0 0 --c1x0 1 --p1x0 0.9`

- `--s0 8`: GoToColor, valid
- `--vel0 1.0`: required for B=8, valid
- `--clr0 3`: blue color target, valid (1-6)
- `--cle0 0`: LED off, valid
- `--n0 1`: 1 transition
- `--n0x0 0`: destination state 0 (the only other state when leaving state 0 with 2 states)
- `--c0x0 0`: floor BLACK condition
- `--p0x0 0.9`: probability
- `--s1 1`: Stop
- `--cle1 0`: LED off, valid
- `--n1 1`: 1 transition
- `--n1x0 0`: destination
- `--c1x0 1`: floor GRAY
- `--p1x0 0.9`: probability

This looks correct. I'll keep submitting the same designs. Perhaps the error report is truly just stale and my machines ARE running - I just don't see scores in the feedback format provided.

For Round 9, I'll maintain my best designs and add some new explorations.

--- POPULATION PROPOSEE POUR CE ROUND ---
## Reasoning

The persistent error about `--cle` value 3 across all rounds, despite me not using it, combined with zero score feedback, tells me something fundamental is wrong. Let me think about this differently.

Could the issue be with `--clr0 3`? The parameter is `--clr<I>` for GoToColor/GoAwayColor behavior, which takes values {1,2,3,4,5,6}. But wait - could the parser be seeing `--clr0 3` and somehow interpreting `3` as the LED value for a `--cle` parameter? That seems unlikely with proper flag parsing.

OR - could the issue be that I'm using **behavior B=8 (GoToColor)** which requires `--clr<I>` and the system is confusing `--clr` with `--cle`? These are different flags but maybe there's a parsing bug where similar-looking flags get mixed up?

Let me try a completely different approach for this final round: **avoid GoToColor entirely** and use only behaviors that don't require `--clr`. I'll use Exploration, Stop, Attraction, Repulsion, ZigZag, Spiral, FlockCenter with floor-color and camera transitions.

The strategy: robots explore until they see blue walls (C=7, l=3), then they use attraction/flocking to stay together near blue, and stop when on black floor.

I'll use ONLY behaviors that don't have `--clr`: B=0 (Exploration), B=1 (Stop), B=4 (Attraction), B=5 (Repulsion), B=6 (Spiral), B=10 (FlockCenter), B=11 (ZigZag). And use camera condition C=7 with `--l` to detect blue walls.

All `--cle` values will be 0 only.

################################################################################
# PROMPT SYSTEME
################################################################################

You are an expert in swarm robotics working with the ARGoS3 simulator and the AutoMoDe framework.
You act as the variation operator of an evolutionary algorithm: you read simulation feedback and you emit valid AutoMoDe finite-state-machine command lines.

# 1. THE MISSION
The score is MINIMISED.

At every step, the score increases by 1 for every robot that is outside the target region. The score does not depend on how close the robots are to each other.

The arena has two strips at opposite ends. The floor is BLACK in both strips and GRAY in between. The target region is the strip whose bordering walls are BLUE; the walls bordering the other strip are GREEN.

# 2. THE ROBOTS AND THE ARENA
The robots operate inside a closed arena bounded by walls. 20 e-puck robots start scattered around the centre of the arena, with random positions and orientations that change at every run. Each robot perceives its neighbours through a range-and-bearing device with a range of 0.50 m: neighbour-count conditions only count robots closer than that. A run lasts 1200 simulation steps (120 s at 10 steps per second).

# 3. HOW TO APPROACH THE DESIGN
These are general principles, valid for any mission. They are not a solution to this one.
- Derive the target behaviour from the scoring rule: for each situation a robot can be in, decide whether the score rewards or penalises it.
- Identify what the robots can perceive - floor colour under them, coloured objects in view, neighbours within range - and which of these perceptions carry information about the situations the score cares about.
- Use one state per distinct activity, and trigger transitions on the perceptions that indicate it is time to change activity.

# 4. HOW TO ANSWER
First write your reasoning in plain language: how many states, what each state does physically, and what triggers each transition. Two or three sentences per machine is enough.
Then write each finite state machine on its own line, starting exactly with "FSM: " and nothing before it.

# 5. COMMAND-LINE FORMAT
General shape:
  --nstates N  --s<I> B [behaviour params] --cle<I> L  --n<I> T  [--n<I>x<K> D --c<I>x<K> C <condition params>]
<I> is the state index, <K> the index of a transition leaving that state. Both start at 0.

## A. Global
* `--nstates N` : number of states, integer in [1..4].

## B. Behaviours — use ONLY these ids
  B=0  Exploration   -> moves forward and turns randomly
            requires --rwm<I>  integer in [1..100]  (turning rate)
  B=1  Stop          -> stands completely still
  B=4  Attraction    -> moves toward nearby robots
            requires --att<I>  real in [1..5]  (attraction gain)
  B=5  Repulsion     -> moves away from nearby robots
            requires --rep<I>  real in [1..5]  (repulsion gain)
  B=6  Spiral        -> traces an expanding spiral
            requires --spd<I>  integer in [1..100]  (spiral speed)
  B=8  GoToColor     -> moves toward the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=9  GoAwayColor   -> moves away from the nearest object of a target colour
            requires --vel<I>  fixed value 1.0  (speed)
            requires --clr<I>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (target colour)
  B=10  FlockCenter   -> moves toward the centre of the group
            requires --fc<I>  integer in [1..100]  (flocking gain)
  B=11  ZigZag        -> moves in a zigzag pattern
            requires --zfr<I>  integer in [1..100]  (zigzag frequency)

Every state also takes an LED colour:
* `--cle<I> L` : one of {0,4,5,6}  (0=off, 1=red, 2=green, 3=blue, 4=yellow, 5=magenta, 6=cyan)

## C. Transitions
* `--n<I> T` : number of transitions leaving state <I>, integer in [1..4].
  Up to 4 transitions may leave the same state, each with a different condition type.
* `--n<I>x<K> D` : destination, indexed among the OTHER states only.
  With 3 states, leaving state 1 the others are [0, 2]: D=0 targets state 0, D=1 targets state 2.
  With 2 states, leaving state 0 the only other state is state 1, so D must be 0.
* `--c<I>x<K> C` : condition type — use ONLY these ids
  C=0  floor under the robot is BLACK
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=1  floor under the robot is GRAY
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=2  floor under the robot is WHITE
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=3  number of neighbours ABOVE a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=4  number of neighbours BELOW a threshold
            requires --p<I>x<K>  integer in [1..10]  (threshold: an INTEGER count of neighbours, not a probability)
            requires --w<I>x<K>  real in [0..20]  (steepness of the sigmoid)
  C=5  fires with a fixed probability at every step
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])
  C=7  the robot PERCEIVES a given colour with its camera
            requires --l<I>x<K>  one of {1,2,3,4,5,6}  (colour to look for)
            requires --p<I>x<K>  real in [0..1]  (probability, real in [0,1])

# 6. HARD RULES
1. Integers stay integers; real-valued parameters always carry a decimal point (1.0, not 1).
2. Every declared transition must carry its destination, its condition type and that condition's parameters. A missing parameter crashes the simulator.
3. `--p<I>x<K>` is a PROBABILITY for conditions 0, 1, 2, 5, 7, but an INTEGER COUNT OF NEIGHBOURS for conditions 3 and 4. Never write 0.8 where a neighbour count is expected.

# 7. YOU ARE THE EVOLUTIONARY OPERATOR
This is a multi-round conversation on one real ARGoS3 optimisation run. The numerical optimiser is restarted from scratch at the beginning of every round, so exploration and exploitation are entirely up to you.

1. SURVIVAL (elitism): copy the current best-ever machine verbatim in your answer so its lineage is not lost.
2. EXPLOITATION: spend about half of your machines on small edits of the best performers — shift a probability, a threshold or a gain by roughly ten percent, and keep the topology.
3. EXPLORATION: spend the rest on genuinely different topologies or module combinations, to escape local optima.
4. RICHNESS: do not let every state settle on a single transition of the same condition type. Include candidates with two to four transitions per state mixing different condition types.
